:
Diagrams for Compton amplitudes.

5.1.1
Same helicity

The diagram in Figure
2
(a)
gives the same helicity amplitude.
We need to compute the following 2-point function of linear
A^{+}
-fluctuation coupling terms (
163
), where we substitute the
A^{+}_{\mu}
fields by the polarisation vectors of the external photons.

\displaystyle\begin{split}iA_{4}^{++}&=\left\langle V^{+}(\varepsilon_{3},k_{3})\times V^{+}(\varepsilon_{4},k_{4})\right\rangle\,,\\
V^{+}(\varepsilon,k)&=-q\int_{k,\omega}\varepsilon_{\mu}^{+}(k)\left[\kappa v^{\mu}k_{\nu}-\omega\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}\right]\delta z^{\nu}(\omega)e^{ik\cdot z_{0}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[\kappa(k\cdot v)-\omega]\,.\end{split}

(201)

Using the
\langle\delta z\delta z\rangle
2-point function (
148
) and contracting the tensor indices, we get

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{++}

\displaystyle=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{i(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot z_{0}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v]

(202)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\left\{\frac{(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot v)(\varepsilon_{4}^{+}\cdot v)}{(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}+\frac{(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot k_{4})(\varepsilon_{4}^{+}\cdot v)-(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot v)(\varepsilon_{4}^{+}\cdot k_{3})}{(k_{4}\cdot v)}-(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot\varepsilon_{4}^{+})\right\}\,.

This is a simple shift of the non-spinning sector results
Kim:2023drc
by an exponential spin factor.
The sign difference compared to (3.26) of the same reference comes from metric conventions. We may also write the amplitude in a gauge-invariant form as

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{++}

\displaystyle=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\frac{(v\cdot F_{3}^{+}\cdot F_{4}^{+}\cdot v)}{(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}e^{i(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot z_{0}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v]\,,

(203)

where
F_{\mu\nu}^{\pm}=i(k_{\mu}\varepsilon_{\nu}^{\pm}-k_{\nu}\varepsilon_{\mu}^{\pm})
is the on-shell field strengths of the external photons and
(v\cdot F_{1}\cdot F_{2}\cdots)=v_{\mu_{1}}F_{1~~\mu_{2}}^{~\mu_{1}}F_{2~~\mu_{3}}^{~\mu_{2}}\cdots
is a shorthand notation for a concatenation of tensor contractions. The expression (
203
) can be obtained directly from the Feynman rules corresponding to the alternative interaction term expansion (
165
).

5.1.2
Opposite helicity

The opposite helicity Compton amplitude comes from
the diagram in Figure
2
(b).
We may simplify the relevant expression in (
148
) as

\displaystyle\langle\delta\bar{z}^{\mu}(\omega^{\prime})\delta z^{\nu}(\omega)\rangle

\displaystyle\approx\frac{2i}{m}\left[\frac{\eta^{\mu\nu}}{2\omega^{2}}+\frac{i\epsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\sigma}v_{\lambda}y_{0\sigma}}{\omega}\right]\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(\omega^{\prime}+\omega)\,.

(204)

We discarded terms proportional to
v^{\mu}
using the fact that the vertex rules at linear order in perturbations satisfy the “Ward identity” and vanish under the substitution
\delta z^{\mu}\to v^{\mu}
.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &iq\int_{k,\omega}A_{\mu}^{+}(k)\left[\kappa v^{\mu}k_{\nu}-\omega\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}\right]\delta z^{\nu}(\omega)e^{ik\cdot z_{0}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[\kappa(k\cdot v)-\omega]\\
&=-i\kappa q\int_{k,\omega}A_{\mu}^{+}(k)(k\cdot v)\left[\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}-\frac{v^{\mu}k_{\nu}}{k\cdot v}\right]\delta z^{\nu}(\omega)e^{ik\cdot z_{0}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[\kappa(k\cdot v)-\omega]\,.\end{aligned}

(205)

Introducing the notation
a^{\mu\nu}=\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}v_{\alpha}y_{0\beta}
, the resulting Compton amplitude becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}iA_{4}^{+-}&=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{i(k_{3}\cdot z_{0}+k_{4}\cdot\bar{z}_{0})}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v]\\
&\phantom{=}\times\left\{\frac{(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot v)(\varepsilon_{4}^{-}\cdot v)}{(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}+\frac{(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot k_{4})(\varepsilon_{4}^{-}\cdot v)-(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot v)(\varepsilon_{4}^{-}\cdot k_{3})}{(k_{4}\cdot v)}\right.\\
&\phantom{=}\left.\phantom{asdf}-(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot\varepsilon_{4}^{-})+\frac{2i(k_{3}\cdot a\cdot k_{4})(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot v)(\varepsilon_{4}^{-}\cdot v)}{(k_{4}\cdot v)}-2i(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot a\cdot\varepsilon_{4}^{-})(k_{4}\cdot v)\right.\\
&\phantom{=}\left.\phantom{\frac{(k_{3}\cdot a\cdot k_{4})}{(k_{4}\cdot v)}}+2i(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot a\cdot k_{4})(\varepsilon_{4}^{-}\cdot v)-2i(k_{3}\cdot a\cdot\varepsilon_{4}^{-})(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot v)\right\}\,.\end{split}

(206)

Similar to (
203
), the amplitude can be written in a gauge-invariant form as

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{+-}

\displaystyle=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\left\{\frac{(v\cdot F_{3}^{+}\cdot F_{4}^{-}\cdot v)}{(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}+2i\frac{(v\cdot F_{3}^{+}\cdot a\cdot F_{4}^{-}\cdot v)}{(k_{4}\cdot v)}\right\}e^{i(k_{3}\cdot z_{0}+k_{4}\cdot\bar{z}_{0})}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v]\,,

(207)

which is more natural when the alternative interaction term expansion (
165
) is used for the Feynman rules.

5.2
Comparison

We compare our classical Compton amplitudes with existing results in the literature, setting
g=2
.
The linear-in-spin amplitude should agree, since it is the universal part captured by the Thomas-Bargmann-Michel-Telegdi (TBMT) equation.
We may find model-dependent discrepancies starting from
the quadratic order.

The deviation at quadratic order in spin is an analogue of possible
R^{2}
type couplings at
\mathcal{O}(S^{4})
in the gravitational case
Bern:2022kto
.
Such curvature-squared type couplings (
R^{2}
or
F^{2}
) have an interpretation as contributions from induced multipole moments, requiring dimensionful coefficients for their correct normalisation;
[ML^{4}]
for gravity and
[M^{-1}L^{2}]
for electromagnetism at the leading order. We can introduce such operators without introducing any additional length scale in the case of spinning objects, since the spin-length vector
a^{\mu}
provides the necessary length scale, the spin order being
\mathcal{O}(S^{4})
for gravity and
\mathcal{O}(S^{2})
for electromagnetism for the leading order curvature-squared operators.

5.2.1
Comparison with SUSY WQFT calculations

To compare the two results (
202
) and (
206
) with those of ref.
Kim:2023drc
, we use the explicit polarisation vectors

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{3\mu}^{+}=\frac{[3|\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}|4\rangle}{\sqrt{2}\langle 34\rangle}\,,\quad\varepsilon_{4\mu}^{-}=\frac{[3|\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}|4\rangle}{\sqrt{2}[43]}\,,\quad\varepsilon_{4\mu}^{+}=\frac{[4|\bar{\sigma}_{\mu}|3\rangle}{\sqrt{2}\langle 43\rangle}\,,

(208)

and the complex conjugation conditions

\displaystyle\left(\lambda_{\alpha}\right)^{\ast}=\text{sgn}(p^{0})\bar{\lambda}_{\dot{\alpha}}\,.

(209)

We use
\text{sgn}(k_{3}^{0})\text{sgn}(k_{4}^{0})=-1
because one of the massless photons has to be ingoing and the other has to be outgoing.
In the rest frame of
v^{\mu}=(1,\vec{0})
where
\omega=k_{3}^{0}
is the energy of the photon,
k^{\mu}=k_{3}^{\mu}+k_{4}^{\mu}
is the transfer momentum, and

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}(k_{4}\cdot v)=-(k_{3}\cdot v)=\omega\,,\,|\langle 34\rangle|^{2}=\langle 34\rangle[34]={k^{2}}=4\omega^{2}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)=|[34]|^{2}\,,\,\\
|[3|v|4\rangle|^{2}={-[3|v|4|v|3\rangle}={4(v\cdot k_{4})^{2}-k^{2}}=4\omega^{2}(1-\sin^{2}(\theta/2))=|[4|v|3\rangle|^{2}\,,\end{gathered}

where
\theta
is the scattering angle and we localised onto
(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v=0
for the second expression.

The same helicity amplitude becomes

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{++}

\displaystyle=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{-k\cdot y_{0}}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)

(212)

which is simple to evaluate because the
\langle 34\rangle^{-2}
factors out in the calculations, which we substitute by
|\langle 34\rangle|^{-2}
. For the opposite helicity amplitude, we get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} iA_{4}^{+-}&=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{(k_{4}-k_{3})\cdot y_{0}}\frac{1}{-2k^{2}}\left\{\frac{k^{2}(n\cdot v)^{2}}{2\omega^{2}}+\frac{2i\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v,y_{0}](n\cdot v)^{2}}{\omega}-2i(k\cdot a\cdot n)(n\cdot v)\right\}\,,\end{aligned}

(213)

where
n^{\mu}=[3|\bar{\sigma}^{\mu}|4\rangle
vector carries the helicity weights. Note that this form is
manifestly shift-symmetric
; the expression is invariant under the shift of the spin vector by

\displaystyle S^{\mu}\to S^{\mu}+\xi k^{\mu}/k^{2}\,,

where
\xi
is an arbitrary parameter. The shift symmetry is one of the conjectures for tensor structures of spinning black holes
Aoude:2022trd
;
Bern:2022kto
.

Now we multiply the factor
\frac{[4|v|3\rangle^{2}}{|[4|v|3\rangle|^{2}}
to compensate the helicity weights, and use the identity for
f^{\mu}=-a^{\mu\nu}k_{\nu}
,

to obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} iA_{4}^{+-}&=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{(k_{4}-k_{3})\cdot y_{0}}\left\{-\cos^{2}(\theta/2)-(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot y_{0}+i\frac{\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v,y_{0}]}{\omega}\right\}\\
&=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\left\{-\cos^{2}(\theta/2)+(\cos^{2}(\theta/2)-1)(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot y_{0}+i\frac{\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v,y_{0}]}{\omega}+\mathcal{O}(y_{0}^{2})\right\}\,.\end{aligned}

(215)

Setting
g=2
, the results in (3.27) of ref.
Kim:2023drc
for
h=1
become
11
11

11

A factor of 2 has been removed from the results of ref.
Kim:2023drc
; the factor is due to overcounting
s
-channel and
u
-channel diagrams.

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{+-}

\displaystyle=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\left[-\cos^{2}(\theta/2)+\sin^{2}(\theta/2)\kappa_{0}[(k_{1}-k_{4})\cdot a]-\frac{i}{\omega}(-\kappa_{1})\epsilon[k_{1},k_{4},a,v]\right]\,,\,

(216a)

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{++}

\displaystyle=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\sin^{2}(\theta/2)\left[1-\kappa_{0}[(k_{1}+k_{4})\cdot a]\right]\,,

(216b)

where
a^{\mu}=S^{\mu}/m
, and we have restored the convention parameters
\kappa_{0}
and
\kappa_{1}
; ref.
Kim:2023drc
uses
\kappa_{0}=\eta_{00}=+1
and
\kappa_{1}=\epsilon_{0123}=-1
. The expressions match perfectly when we set
k_{1}^{\mu}=k_{3}^{\mu}
and
y_{0}^{\mu}=-a^{\mu}
.

5.2.2
Comparison with amplitude calculations

For the spin squared coupling, we compare our result with the minimal coupling amplitude constructed from BCFW recursion
Chen:2021kxt

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{4}^{+-}&\propto\frac{[3|p_{1}|4\rangle^{2}}{(s-m^{2})(u-m^{2})}\exp\left[-i\frac{k^{\mu}[3|\sigma^{\nu}|4\rangle S_{\mu\nu}}{\kappa_{0}[3|p_{1}|4\rangle}\right]\,,\end{aligned}

(217)

where we have restored the metric convention parameter
\kappa_{0}
.
Matching the overall normalisation
12
12

12

As remarked in ref.
Kim:2023drc
, there is a mass factor difference between QFT amplitude results and WQFT amplitude results, which can be interpreted as the ratio of
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v)
to
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(2k\cdot p)
.
we write the amplitude as

\displaystyle iA_{4}^{+-}

\displaystyle=-\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\frac{(n\cdot v)^{2}}{4(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}\exp\left[i\frac{\epsilon[k_{3}+k_{4},n,v,a]}{(n\cdot v)}\right]\,,

(218)

where we set
p_{1}^{\mu}=mv^{\mu}
and take the classical limit for the Mandelstam invariants as

\displaystyle s-m^{2}=-(u-m^{2})+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})=2m(k_{4}\cdot v)+\mathcal{O}(\hbar^{2})\,.

(219)

The exponent can be simplified to

\displaystyle\frac{i\epsilon[k_{3}+k_{4},n,v,a]}{(n\cdot v)}=(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot a-\frac{[(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot v](n\cdot a)}{(n\cdot v)}

(220)

using the identity

\displaystyle i\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\sigma}=\frac{(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\sigma^{\lambda}\bar{\sigma}^{\sigma})_{\alpha}{}^{\alpha}-(\sigma^{\nu}\bar{\sigma}^{\lambda}\sigma^{\sigma}\bar{\sigma}^{\mu})_{\alpha}{}^{\alpha}}{-4}

(221)

and Schouten identities.
It leads to the form of the amplitude used in HPET/HEFT
Aoude:2022trd
;
Aoude:2022thd
;
Aoude:2023vdk
and BHPT
Bautista:2021wfy
;
Bautista:2022wjf
approaches,

\displaystyle i(A_{4}^{+-})_{\mathrm{HEFT}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\frac{(n\cdot v)^{2}}{4(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}e^{(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot a}\exp\left[\frac{2(k_{4}\cdot v)(n\cdot a)}{(n\cdot v)}\right]

(222)

after localising onto
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v]
, where the amplitude is regular up to order
\mathcal{O}(a^{2})
. We compare it to the classical Compton amplitude of our twistor model,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} iA_{4}^{+-}&=\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot a}\left\{-\frac{(n\cdot v)^{2}}{4(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}+\frac{i\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v,a](n\cdot v)^{2}}{2(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})(k_{4}\cdot v)}-\frac{i\epsilon[k_{3}+k_{4},n,v,a](n\cdot v)}{2(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})}\right\}\\
&=-\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\frac{(n\cdot v)^{2}}{4(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}e^{(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot a}\left\{1-\frac{2(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}{(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})}\left[\frac{i\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v,a]}{(k_{4}\cdot v)}-\frac{i\epsilon[k_{3}+k_{4},n,v,a]}{(n\cdot v)}\right]\right\}\end{aligned}

where we used
y_{0}^{\mu}=-a^{\mu}
. We can use the identities (
k=k_{3}+k_{4}
)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (v\cdot n)\epsilon[k,k_{4},v,a]&=(v\cdot k)\epsilon[n,k_{4},v,a]+(v\cdot k_{4})\epsilon[k,n,v,a]\\
&\phantom{=asdf}+(v\cdot v)\epsilon[k,k_{4},n,a]+(v\cdot a)\epsilon[k,k_{4},v,n]\\
&=(k_{4}\cdot v)\epsilon[k,n,v,a]-\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},n,a]\,,\end{aligned}

(223)

and

\displaystyle i\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},n,a]

\displaystyle=(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})(n\cdot a)\,,

(224)

to write the twistor Compton amplitude as

\displaystyle i(A_{4}^{+-})_{\mathrm{twistor}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{iq^{2}}{m}\frac{(n\cdot v)^{2}}{4(k_{4}\cdot v)^{2}}e^{(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot a}\left\{1+\frac{2(k_{4}\cdot v)(n\cdot a)}{(n\cdot v)}\right\}\,.

(225)

The difference between (
222
) and (
225
) is a non-pole-possessing term,
13
13

13

Adding (
226
) (without
\mathcal{O}(a^{3})
corrections) to (
225
) results in the HPET amplitude (3.9) of ref.
Aoude:2022trd
with all free parameters
c_{j}^{(n)}
set to zero. JWK would like to thank Kays Haddad for pointing this out.

\displaystyle i\Delta A_{4}^{+-}

\displaystyle=-\frac{iq^{2}}{m}e^{(k_{3}-k_{4})\cdot a}(n\cdot a)^{2}+\mathcal{O}(a^{3})\,.

(226)

This term can be reverse-engineered to find a
\mathcal{O}(a^{2})
worldline contact term that generates this contribution. From the definition of the polarisation vector (
208
) we relate the
n^{\mu}
vectors to the polarisation vectors as

\displaystyle n^{\mu}n^{\nu}=-4(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})\varepsilon_{3}^{+\mu}\varepsilon_{4}^{-\nu}\,.

(227)

To ensure the Ward identity, we use the substitution rule

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} n^{\mu}n^{\nu}&\to-4\left[(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})\varepsilon_{3}^{+\mu}\varepsilon_{4}^{-\nu}-(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot k_{4})k_{3}^{\mu}\varepsilon_{4}^{-\nu}-(k_{3}\cdot\varepsilon_{4}^{-})\varepsilon_{3}^{+\mu}k_{4}^{\nu}+(\varepsilon_{3}^{+}\cdot\varepsilon_{4}^{-})k_{3}^{\mu}k_{4}^{\nu}\right]\end{aligned}

(228)

with symmetrisation if necessary. We may also write this substitution as

\displaystyle n_{\mu}n_{\nu}

\displaystyle\to 4\eta^{\alpha\beta}F_{\alpha\mu}^{+}(k_{3})F_{\beta\nu}^{-}(k_{4})\,,\,F_{\mu\nu}^{\pm}(k):=ik_{\mu}\varepsilon^{\pm}_{\nu}(k)-ik_{\nu}\varepsilon^{\pm}_{\mu}(k)\,,

(229)

where
F^{\pm}_{\mu\nu}(k)
is the momentum space mode coefficient of the field strength 2-form
F^{\pm}=dA^{\pm}
.
We can now attribute the difference (
226
) to the worldline contact term

\displaystyle iS_{\text{cont}}

\displaystyle=i\int\frac{4q^{2}}{m}\left[y\cdot F^{+}(z)\cdot F^{-}(\bar{z})\cdot y\right]d\sigma+\mathcal{O}(y^{3})\,.

(230)

As we observed in section
2.2.2
, the combination
(y\cdot F^{+}\cdot F^{-}\cdot y)
is an inevitable consequence of the zig-zag symplectic perturbation theory. Our twistor model differs from other models which do not carry
(y\cdot F^{+}\cdot F^{-}\cdot y)
terms.

The Compton amplitude (
225
) can also be compared to predictions of higher-spin gauge symmetry
Cangemi:2023ysz
. In the notations of ref.
Cangemi:2023ysz
, the non-scalar part of (
225
) can be written as
e^{x}(1-w)
, which differs from the result (6.61) of ref.
Cangemi:2023ysz
reproduced below

\displaystyle e^{x}\cosh z-we^{x}\frac{\sinh z}{z}+\frac{w^{2}-z^{2}}{2}E(x,y,z)\,,

(231)

where

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}x=-(k_{4}-k_{3})\cdot a\,,\,y=-(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot a\,,\,z=-|a|v_{1}\cdot(k_{4}-k_{3})\,,\\
w=-\frac{(n\cdot a)[v\cdot(k_{4}-k_{3})]}{(n\cdot v)}\,,\end{gathered}

and

\displaystyle E(x,y,z)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{y}-e^{x}\cosh z+(x-y)e^{x}\frac{\sinh z}{z}}{(x-y)^{2}-z^{2}}+(y\to-y)\,.

While this amplitude is quite different from the twistor worldline prediction (
225
), the amplitude shares the same
e^{x}
factor conjectured to be responsible for the singularity structure of the aligned-spin one-loop eikonal (
260
).

6
Conservative dynamics from WQFT

In this section, we revisit the scattering observables of the binary system from the WQFT perspective.
We computed the 1PL and 2PL observables in section
3
by solving the equations of motion
and extracted the classical eikonal along the way.
One advantage of the WQFT approach is that it allows us to compute the eikonal before computing observables.
Using two approaches to compute the same eikonal serves as a consistency check.
Besides, we specialise to the aligned spin configurations and evaluate the Fourier integrals explicitly
to obtain the position space expressions for the classical eikonal up to the 2PL order.

6.1
1PL observables and eikonal

Since the complex coordinate
z^{\mu}=x^{\mu}+iy^{\mu}
includes the position and the spin (recall the sign
a^{\mu}=-y^{\mu}
),
we can compute the velocity kick
\Delta v^{\mu}
and the spin kick
\Delta y^{\mu}
both from the expectation values
\langle\!\langle\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\rangle\!\rangle
and
\langle\!\langle\delta\bar{z}_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\rangle\!\rangle
.
For particle 1, the expectation value
\langle\!\langle\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\rangle\!\rangle
is given by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \langle\!\langle\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\rangle\!\rangle&=-q_{1}q_{2}\int_{k,k^{\prime},\omega^{\prime}}\left[\langle\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\delta z_{1}^{\nu}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle\langle F_{\nu\lambda}^{+}(k)A_{\alpha}^{-}(k^{\prime})\rangle e^{ik\cdot z_{1}+ik^{\prime}\cdot\bar{z}_{2}}\right.\\
&\phantom{=asdfasdfasdfasdf}\left.{}+\langle\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\delta\bar{z}_{1}^{\nu}(\omega^{\prime})\rangle\langle F_{\nu\lambda}^{-}(k)A_{\alpha}^{+}(k^{\prime})\rangle e^{ik\cdot\bar{z}_{1}+ik^{\prime}\cdot{z}_{2}}\right]\\
&\phantom{=asdfasdfasdfasdfasdfasdf}\times v_{1}^{\lambda}v_{2}^{\alpha}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k\cdot v_{1})-\omega^{\prime}]\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k^{\prime}\cdot v_{2})\,,\end{aligned}

(232)

where
z_{1,2}
,
\bar{z}_{1,2}
in the exponents are understood as background values.
This expression is free of Dirac string singularity when we use the propagator (
188
).

The velocity kick and the spin kick can be computed from the expectation values as

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1)}v_{1}^{\mu}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{Re}}\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\frac{d\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\tau)}{d\tau}={\operatorname{Re}}\int_{\tau}\frac{d^{2}\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\tau)}{d\tau^{2}}={\operatorname{Re}}\int_{\tau,\omega}(-\omega^{2})\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)e^{-i\omega\tau}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{Re}}\lim_{\omega\to 0}(-\omega^{2})\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\,,

(233)

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1)}y_{1}^{\mu}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{Im}}\lim_{\tau\to\infty}\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\tau)={\operatorname{Im}}\int_{\tau}\frac{d\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\tau)}{d\tau}={\operatorname{Im}}\int_{\tau,\omega}(-i\omega)\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)e^{-i\omega\tau}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{Im}}\lim_{\omega\to 0}(-i\omega)\delta z_{1}^{\mu}(\omega)\,.

(234)

This may be viewed as the worldline version of the LSZ reduction formula
Lehmann:1954rq
; we can expect that the equivalent of the
S
-matrix equivalence theorem
Chisholm:1961tha
;
Kamefuchi:1961sb
will also hold for worldline observables in WQFT.
After some algebra, we can write the velocity kick as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Delta_{(1)}v_{1}^{\mu}&=-\frac{q_{1}q_{2}}{2m_{1}}\int_{k}\frac{i\gamma k^{\mu}-\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{1},v_{2}]}{k^{2}}e^{k\cdot y}e^{ik\cdot b}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{2})\\
&\phantom{=}-\frac{q_{1}q_{2}}{2m_{1}}\int_{k}\frac{i\gamma k^{\mu}+\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{1},v_{2}]}{k^{2}}e^{-k\cdot y}e^{ik\cdot b}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{2})\,.\end{aligned}

(235)

Similarly, we can write the spin kick as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Delta_{(1)}y_{1}^{\mu}&=+\frac{q_{1}q_{2}}{2m_{1}}\int_{k}\frac{i(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})k^{\mu}-i(k\cdot y_{1})v_{2}^{\mu}-v_{1}^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]-\gamma\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{1},y_{1}]}{k^{2}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times e^{ik\cdot(b-iy)}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{2})\\
&\phantom{=}+\frac{q_{1}q_{2}}{2m_{1}}\int_{k}\frac{i(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})k^{\mu}-i(k\cdot y_{1})v_{2}^{\mu}+v_{1}^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]+\gamma\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{1},y_{1}]}{k^{2}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times e^{ik\cdot(b+iy)}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{2})\,.\end{aligned}

(236)

Both
\Delta_{(1)}v_{1}^{\mu}
and
\Delta_{(1)}y_{1}^{\mu}

agree with the results of section
3
as expected.

1PL eikonal

{fmfgraph*}
(40,60)

\fmfstraight

\fmfright

\fmfv
decor.shape=square,decor.filled=shaded,decor.size=10a1

\fmfv
decor.shape=square,decor.filled=empty,decor.size=10a2

\fmf
wiggly,tension=0a1,a2

Figure 3
:
Diagrams contributing to the 1PL eikonal.

The eikonal is evaluated as the sum over the diagrams in Figure
3
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} i\chi_{(1)}&=-q_{1}q_{2}\int_{k_{\perp}}v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}\left[e^{ik\cdot(z_{1}-\bar{z}_{2})}\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)+e^{ik\cdot(\bar{z}_{1}-{z}_{2})}\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]\,,\end{aligned}

(237)

where
\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)
and
\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)
are the helicity propagators (
184
).
We write the integrand as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}\left[e^{-k\cdot y}\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)+e^{+k\cdot y}\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]\\
&\qquad=\cosh(k\cdot y)v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}\left[\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)+\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]\\
&\qquad\quad\phantom{=}+\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)}y^{\lambda}v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}(ik_{\lambda})\left[i\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)-i\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]\,.\end{aligned}

(238)

Using (
186
), we can simplify the
(\cosh)
term slightly and write

\displaystyle\cosh(k\cdot y)v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}\left[\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)+\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]=\cosh(k\cdot y)(v_{1}\cdot v_{2})\frac{-i}{k^{2}}\,.

(239)

We remind the readers that we are treating terms proportional to
(v_{1}\cdot k)
,
(v_{2}\cdot k)

or
k^{2}
as zero.
The
(\sinh)
term is more interesting.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &y^{\lambda}v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}(ik_{\lambda})\left[i\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)-i\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]\\
&=y^{\lambda}v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}(ik_{\lambda})\left[i\Delta^{+-}_{\mu\nu}(k)-i\Delta^{-+}_{\mu\nu}(k)\right]-y^{\lambda}v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}(ik_{\mu})\left[i\Delta^{+-}_{\lambda\nu}(k)-i\Delta^{-+}_{\lambda\nu}(k)\right]\\
&=y^{\lambda}v_{1}^{\mu}v_{2}^{\nu}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu}{}^{\alpha\beta}(ik_{\alpha})\left[\Delta^{+-}_{\beta\nu}(k)+\Delta^{-+}_{\beta\nu}(k)\right]=i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{-i}{k^{2}}\,.\end{aligned}

(240)

The term added to the second line vanishes due to
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{1}\cdot k)
; its purpose is to anti-symmetrise in
\lambda
,
\mu
indices.
The equality between the second line and the third line follows from (
186
) and the 4d Schouten identity (
105
).
In the end, we obtain

\displaystyle\chi_{(1)}

\displaystyle=-q_{1}q_{2}\int_{k_{\perp}}\left[\cosh(k\cdot y)\gamma-i\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\right]\frac{e^{ik\cdot b}}{k^{2}}\,.

(241)

in agreement with (
103
).
It is free of the Dirac string ambiguity as expected.

Next, we perform the Fourier integral explicitly and obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\chi_{(1)}&=\frac{q_{1}q_{2}\gamma}{4\pi\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}+{\operatorname{Re}}\left(\log\frac{(b^{\mu}+iy_{\perp}^{\mu})^{2}}{b_{0}^{2}}\right)\phantom{+\left(\frac{y_{\perp}^{2}+\sqrt{y_{\perp}^{2}-(b\cdot y_{\perp})^{2}}}{y_{\perp}^{2}-\sqrt{y_{\perp}^{2}-(b\cdot y_{\perp})^{2}}}\right)}\right.\\
&\phantom{=asdf}\left.\phantom{asdf}-\frac{\epsilon[b,v_{1},v_{2},y_{\perp}]}{2\gamma\sqrt{b^{2}y_{\perp}^{2}-(b\cdot y_{\perp})^{2}}}\log\left(\frac{b^{2}+y_{\perp}^{2}+2\sqrt{b^{2}y_{\perp}^{2}-(b\cdot y_{\perp})^{2}}}{b^{2}+y_{\perp}^{2}-2\sqrt{b^{2}y_{\perp}^{2}-(b\cdot y_{\perp})^{2}}}\right)\right]\,,\end{split}

where
D=4-2\epsilon
,
b_{0}^{2}
is the dimensional regularisation parameter absorbing all regularisation artefacts (factors of
\pi
and
\gamma_{E}
, etc.), and

\displaystyle y_{\perp}^{\mu}=y^{\mu}+\left(\frac{\gamma(y\cdot v_{2})-(y\cdot v_{1})}{\gamma^{2}-1}\right)v_{1}^{\mu}+\left(\frac{\gamma(y\cdot v_{1})-(y\cdot v_{2})}{\gamma^{2}-1}\right)v_{2}^{\mu}

(242)

is the projection of
y^{\mu}
onto the impact parameter space defined by
b\cdot v_{1}=b\cdot v_{2}=0
.
In the aligned spin configuration,
y_{\perp}^{\mu}=y^{\mu}
and
(b\cdot y)=0
, the eikonal simplifies even further,

\displaystyle\chi_{(1,\text{aligned})}

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{1}q_{2}\gamma}{4\pi\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\left[\frac{1}{\epsilon}+\log\frac{b^{2}-y^{2}}{b_{0}^{2}}-\frac{\epsilon[b,v_{1},v_{2},y]}{\gamma|b||y|}\log\left(\frac{|b|+|y|}{|b|-|y|}\right)\right]\,,

(243)

where
|b|=\sqrt{b^{2}}
and
|y|=\sqrt{y^{2}}
. Note that the aligned spin eikonal develops a logarithmic singularity at
b^{2}=y^{2}
; the eikonal “knows” that classical spin is a finite-size effect and the point particle approximation breaks down when the two bodies are too close to each other.

.

Diagrams contributing to the 2PL eikonal, up to the exchange of the two particles and the overall flip of holomorphy/helicity.

Building up the eikonal integrand

The integrand can be constructed by replacing the field strength tensors
F^{\pm}_{i\mu\nu}
of the Compton amplitudes (
203
) and (
207
) by the linearised source contribution from the other particle using the propagator (
188
),

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} F_{i\mu\nu}^{+}&\to\frac{(k\wedge v_{2})_{\mu\nu}-i\epsilon_{\mu\nu}[k_{i},v_{2}]}{2k_{i}^{2}}\times iq_{2}e^{-ik_{i}\cdot\bar{z}_{2}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k_{i}\cdot v_{2})\,,\\
F_{i\mu\nu}^{-}&\to\frac{(k\wedge v_{2})_{\mu\nu}+i\epsilon_{\mu\nu}[k_{i},v_{2}]}{2k_{i}^{2}}\times iq_{2}e^{-ik_{i}\cdot{z}_{2}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k_{i}\cdot v_{2})\,,\end{aligned}

(244)

attaching symmetry factors, integrating over photon momenta
\int_{k_{3},k_{4}}
, summing over helicity configurations, and summing over worldline permutation
1\leftrightarrow 2
.

\displaystyle i\chi_{(2)}

\displaystyle=i\int_{k_{3},k_{4}}\left[I^{++}+2I^{+-}+I^{--}\right]+(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(245)

where we used the fact that
I^{+-}=I^{-+}
.

Same helicity integrand

The integrand turns out to be quite simple

\displaystyle I^{++}=-\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{8m_{1}}e^{i(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot(z_{1}-\bar{z}_{2})}\frac{\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{1}\cdot k_{3}+v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{3})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{4})}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})}\frac{k_{3}\cdot k_{4}}{k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}\,.

(246)

At the level of the integrand, it is clear that
I^{++}+I^{--}
agrees with the
\cosh[(k+\ell)\cdot y]
term in (
118
) upon the identification
(k_{3},k_{4})_{\mathrm{here}}\leftrightarrow(k,\ell)_{\mathrm{there}}
.

We can use Passarino-Veltman reduction to rewrite the integrand as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{k_{3}\cdot k_{4}}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}&=\frac{1}{2}\left[\frac{(k_{3}+k_{4})^{2}}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}-\frac{k_{3}^{2}+k_{4}^{2}}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}\right]\\
&=\frac{-1}{2}\left[\frac{k^{2}}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}}-\frac{2}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}k_{3}^{2}}\right]\end{aligned}

(247)

where we used the symmetry between
k_{3}
and
k_{4}
, and then used the condition
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v_{1}]
.
We also set
k^{\mu}=k_{3}^{\mu}+k_{4}^{\mu}
. We are left with evaluation of the integral (in
D=4-2\epsilon
dimensions for regularisation)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{I}^{++}(k,v)&:=\int_{k_{3},k_{4}}\frac{k_{3}\cdot k_{4}\,e^{i(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot z}}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v_{1}]\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt[(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot v_{2}]\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k_{3}\cdot v_{2})\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{k_{\perp}}\,e^{ik\cdot z}\int_{k_{3}}\left[\frac{k^{2}}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}}-\frac{2}{(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}k_{3}^{2}}\right]\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k_{3}\cdot v_{2})\,.\end{aligned}

(248)

Then we separate the integral argument into
k_{3\parallel}
and
k_{3\perp}
, such that
k_{3\perp}\cdot v_{2}=0
. The
dk_{3\parallel}
integral is trivial due to
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k_{3}\cdot v_{2})
, and we get

\displaystyle\int d^{D-1}k_{3\perp}\left[\frac{k^{2}}{(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3\perp})^{2}k_{3\perp}^{2}(k-k_{3\perp})^{2}}-\frac{2}{(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3\perp})^{2}k_{3\perp}^{2}}\right]\,,

(249)

where
v_{1\perp}^{\mu}=v_{1}^{\mu}+(v_{1}\cdot v_{2})v_{2}^{\mu}=v_{1}^{\mu}-\gamma v_{2}^{\mu}
is the projection of
v_{1}^{\mu}
onto the orthogonal space such that
v_{1\perp}\cdot v_{2}=0
. The projection for
k^{\mu}
is not needed due to
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{2})
constraint. The remaining integrals evaluate to zero when using the master integral (
285
), which is consistent with vanishing same helicity sector contributions for the triangle coefficient in amplitude calculations
Guevara:2017csg
;
Guevara:2018wpp
;
Chung:2018kqs
.

Opposite helicity integrand

Evaluating the relevant diagrams, and factoring out the common denominator as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} I^{+-}&=-\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{8m_{1}}e^{i(k_{3}+k_{4})\cdot b}\frac{\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{1}\cdot k_{3}+v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{3})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{4})}{k_{3}^{2}k_{4}^{2}(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})}\\
&\phantom{=asdfasdf}\times e^{(k_{4}-k_{3})\cdot(y_{1}+y_{2})}N[k_{3},k_{4},v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\,,\end{aligned}

(250)

we find that the numerator, organized in powers of
\gamma
, is given as

\displaystyle N

\displaystyle=2\gamma^{2}\left(i(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v_{1},y_{1}]+(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})\right)

(251)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2i\gamma\left(\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v_{1},v_{2}]-2i(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})^{2}\epsilon[(k_{3}+k_{4}),v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-(k_{3}\cdot k_{4})+2(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})^{2}\left[1+(y_{1}\cdot k_{3})-(y_{1}\cdot k_{4})\right]+2i(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\epsilon[k_{3},k_{4},v_{1},v_{2}]\,.

It is straightforward to show that it agrees with the opposite helicity integrand in (
118
).

To evaluate the Fourier integrals, it is convenient to reorganise the numerator as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} N&=\gamma^{2}\left[k^{2}-k_{3}^{2}-k_{4}^{2}\right]+2i\gamma^{2}(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\epsilon[k_{3},k,v_{1},y_{1}]\\
&\phantom{=}+2i\gamma\epsilon[k_{3},k,v_{1},v_{2}]+2\gamma(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\left[k^{2}-k_{3}^{2}-k_{4}^{2}\right]\\
&\phantom{=}+2i\gamma(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\\
&\phantom{=}-\frac{1}{2}\left[k^{2}-k_{3}^{2}-k_{4}^{2}\right]-2(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})\left[1+(y_{1}\cdot k_{3})-(y_{1}\cdot k_{4})\right]\\
&\phantom{=}+2i(v_{1}\cdot k_{4})(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\epsilon[k_{3},k,v_{1},v_{2}]\,.\end{aligned}

(252)

Inspecting the master integral (
285
) we find that
k_{3}^{2}
and
k_{4}^{2}
of the numerator will evaluate to zero (
\lambda_{1}=0
or
\lambda_{2}=0
condition) and can be thrown away.
We organise the integrand as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} I^{+-}&=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{8m_{1}}e^{ik\cdot(b-iy)}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{1}\cdot k)\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k)\frac{e^{-2k_{3}\cdot y}N[k_{3},k-k_{3},v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{3})}{k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}}\,,\\
y^{\mu}&=y_{1}^{\mu}+y_{2}^{\mu}\,,\end{aligned}

(253)

where we use
k_{3}
as the loop momentum.
Performing the
dk_{3\parallel}=dk_{3}^{0}
integral we get

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}k_{3}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{e^{-2k_{3}\cdot y}N\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{3})}{k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{d^{D-1}k_{3\perp}}{(2\pi)^{D-1}}\frac{e^{-2k_{3}\cdot y_{\perp}}N_{\perp}}{k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3})^{2}}

(254)

where
y_{\perp}^{\mu}=y_{1}^{\mu}+y_{2}^{\mu}+v_{2}^{\mu}(y_{1}\cdot v_{2})
and the effective numerator is

\displaystyle N_{\perp}

\displaystyle=\left[\gamma^{2}-1/2\right]k^{2}-2\gamma(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})k^{2}(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3})

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2\left[\frac{i\gamma\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]}{\gamma^{2}-1}+1\right](v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3})^{2}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+4(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3})^{2}\left(\left[y_{1}-\frac{k(k\cdot y_{1})}{k^{2}}+v_{2}(y_{1}\cdot v_{2})\right]\cdot k_{3}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-2i\left\{\gamma^{2}\left(\left[\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{1},y_{1}]+\frac{v_{1\perp}^{\mu}\gamma\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]}{\gamma^{2}-1}\right]+v_{2\mu}\epsilon[v_{2},k,v_{1},y_{1}]\right)\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=asdfasdf}\left.\phantom{\left(\left[\frac{v_{1\perp}^{\mu}}{\gamma^{2}}\right]\right)}+(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\epsilon_{\mu}[k,v_{1},v_{2}]\right\}(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3})k_{3}^{\mu}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+2i\gamma\epsilon_{\mu}[k,v_{1},v_{2}]k_{3}^{\mu}\,.

(255)

The remaining integral can be evaluated using the list of integrals in appendix
B.1
. We present the results in the ancillary file
loopdata.dat.m
.
Including the overall
e^{+k\cdot y}
factor from (
253
), the integral is consistent with the QED amplitude coefficients provided by ref.
Bern:2023ity
to bilinear order in spins, under the conditions
C_{i}=1
,
D_{i}=0
, and covariant SSC. We also present the full eikonal as a formal power series in the ancillary file
eikonaldata.dat.m
. While the expressions by themselves do not provide any insight, they greatly simplify in the aligned spin configuration, which we present next.

6.2.1
Aligned spin

Let us simplify the expression by going to the aligned spin configuration. For aligned spin we have the conditions
y_{1}^{\mu}\propto y_{2}^{\mu}
and
y\cdot v_{1,2}=0
. We introduce the ratio parameter
\zeta
defined by
y_{1}^{\mu}=\zeta y^{\mu}
; it follows that
y_{2}^{\mu}=(1-\zeta)y^{\mu}
and
y_{\perp}^{\mu}=y^{\mu}
. This reduces the expression to a single infinite sum and we get

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int\frac{d^{4}k_{3}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{e^{-2k_{3}\cdot y}N\,\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k_{3})}{k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}(v_{1}\cdot k_{3})^{2}}=\int\frac{d^{3}k_{3\perp}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{e^{-2k_{3}\cdot y_{\perp}}N_{\perp}}{k_{3}^{2}(k-k_{3})^{2}(v_{1\perp}\cdot k_{3})^{2}}\\
&=\frac{e^{-k\cdot y}}{4(k^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-\frac{k^{2}y^{2}}{2}\right)^{m}}{m!}\left[\left(\frac{2\gamma^{2}-1}{\gamma^{2}-1}-\zeta\right)\frac{I_{m+1}(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{m-1}}\right.\\
&\phantom{=}\left.\phantom{\left(\frac{2\gamma^{2}}{\gamma^{2}}\right)}+\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}(4m+1)-2m-1}{\gamma^{2}-1}-2m\zeta+\frac{i\gamma(\zeta-2)\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]}{(\gamma^{2}-1)}\right)\frac{I_{m}(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{m}}\right]\,,\end{aligned}

(256)

where
I_{n}(x)
is the modified Bessel function of the first kind. The impact parameter space integral can be organised as

\displaystyle\chi^{+-}

\displaystyle=\int_{k_{3},k_{4}}I^{+-}=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{32m_{1}}\int\frac{d^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{4}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{1}\cdot k)\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot k)\frac{\mathcal{I}^{+-}e^{ik\cdot b}}{\sqrt{k^{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{32m_{1}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\int\frac{d^{2}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\mathcal{I}^{+-}e^{i(k_{E}\cdot b)}}{\sqrt{k_{E}^{2}}}\,,

(257)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{+-}

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\left(-\frac{k^{2}y^{2}}{2}\right)^{m}}{m!}\left[\left(\frac{2\gamma^{2}-1}{\gamma^{2}-1}-\zeta\right)\frac{I_{m+1}(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{m-1}}\right.

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\left.\phantom{as}+\left(\frac{\gamma^{2}(4m+1)-2m-1}{\gamma^{2}-1}-2m\zeta+\frac{i\gamma(\zeta-2)\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]}{(\gamma^{2}-1)}\right)\frac{I_{m}(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{m}}\right]\,,

(258)

where the factor
i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]
can be traded for the derivative operator
\epsilon^{\mu}[v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\partial}{\partial b^{\mu}}
. Imposing the additional constraint
14
14

14

This additional constraint conforms to the usage of “aligned spin” in the literature, where the orbital angular momentum is also aligned with the spin direction.

y\cdot b=0
simplifies the expression further and yields,

\displaystyle\chi^{+-}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{64m_{1}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\pi(b^{2}-y^{2})^{3/2}}\left[b^{2}+\frac{\gamma^{2}(1-\zeta)+\zeta}{\gamma^{2}-1}\,y^{2}\right]\right.

\displaystyle\left.\phantom{asdf\frac{1-(1+2\zeta)(\frac{y^{2}}{b^{2}})}{\pi\sqrt{b^{2}}}}+\frac{(\zeta-2)\gamma}{\pi(\gamma^{2}-1)}\epsilon^{\mu}[v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\partial}{\partial b^{\mu}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{b^{2}-y^{2}}}\right)\,,

(259)

which has a singularity structure
(b^{2}-y^{2})^{-3/2}=(b^{2}-(a_{1}+a_{2})^{2})^{-3/2}
that was not visible in the original perturbative spin expansion. The full aligned-spin 2PL eikonal is

\displaystyle\chi_{(2,\text{aligned})}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}\left(b^{2}-\frac{(\zeta-2)\gamma}{(\gamma^{2}-1)}\,\epsilon[b,v_{1},v_{2},y]+\frac{\gamma^{2}(1-\zeta)+\zeta}{\gamma^{2}-1}\,y^{2}\right)}{32\pi m_{1}\sqrt{\gamma^{2}-1}\,(b^{2}-y^{2})^{3/2}}+\left(1\leftrightarrow 2\right)\,,

(260)

where symmetrisation is implemented by
\{m_{1}\to m_{2}\,,\,v_{1}^{\mu}\leftrightarrow v_{2}^{\mu}\,,\,b^{\mu}\to-b^{\mu}\,,\,\zeta\to 1-\zeta\}
.

We remark that the singularity is still present in the spinless probe limit
\zeta\to 0
, and since spin-dependence of the eikonal integrand (
253
) enters only through the exponential factors
e^{+k\cdot y}e^{-2k_{3}\cdot y}
in this limit, the singularity structure
(b^{2}-y^{2})^{-3/2}
seems to be a consequence of the “Newman-Janis shift” of the integrand, which shifts the displacement between the two worldlines by an imaginary spin sum vector
\pm iy^{\mu}=\mp i(a_{1}^{\mu}+a_{2}^{\mu})
. Note that similar singularity structures at 2PM aligned-spin scattering were reported in the gravitational case; the spinning-spinless eikonal has the form
(b^{2}-a^{2})^{-3/2}

Aoude:2022thd
,
15
15

15

JWK would like to thank Kays Haddad for bringing this reference to attention.
and Kerr-(spinning) probe eikonal has the form
a_{p}^{k}(b^{2}-a_{b}^{2})^{-3/2-k}
where
a_{b}
is the spin parameter of the Kerr background and
a_{p}
is the probe spin (
k\leq 2
)
Damgaard:2022jem
16
16

16

Ref.
Damgaard:2022jem
reports the scattering angle which scales as
a_{p}^{k}(b^{2}-a_{b}^{2})^{-5/2-k}\sim\partial_{b}[a_{p}^{k}(b^{2}-a_{b}^{2})^{-3/2-k}]
.
which can be viewed as an artifact of expanding the singularity
(b^{2}-(a_{b}+a_{p})^{2})^{-3/2}
. If the “Newman-Janis shift” of the integrand persists at higher loop orders, we can conjecture that the singularity structure of the spinless probe scattering
(b^{2}-a_{b}^{2})^{-3n/2}
from
n
-loop contributions reported by ref.
Damgaard:2022jem
generalises to the singularity structure
(b^{2}-(a_{b}+a_{p})^{2})^{-3n/2}
. As remarked when comparing twistor worldline Compton amplitudes with that of higher spin gauge symmetry
Cangemi:2023ysz
, it would also be interesting to check whether same singularity structures appear in the eikonal when the Compton amplitude has exponential dependence on spin, a feature that is also shared by the Compton amplitude construction in ref.
Bjerrum-Bohr:2023iey
.

We also consider axial scattering
y^{\mu}\propto b^{\mu}
, which is independent of the sign of
y\cdot b
because the Fourier integrand (
258
) contains only even powers of
k\cdot y
. The
i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]
contribution drops out due to the condition
y^{\mu}\propto b^{\mu}
, and the Fourier transform (
257
) evaluates to

\displaystyle\chi^{+-}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}\,\sqrt{b^{2}}}{32\pi^{2}m_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1)^{3/2}}\left[\frac{\gamma^{2}(\zeta-1)-\zeta}{b^{2}}K\left(-\frac{y^{2}}{b^{2}}\right)-\frac{\gamma^{2}(\zeta-2)-(\zeta-1)}{b^{2}+y^{2}}E\left(-\frac{y^{2}}{b^{2}}\right)\right]\,,

(261)

where
K(x)
and
E(x)
are the complete elliptic integrals of the first and second kind. The full result is

\displaystyle\chi_{(2,\text{axial})}

\displaystyle=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}\,\sqrt{b^{2}}}{16\pi^{2}m_{1}(\gamma^{2}-1)^{3/2}}\left[\frac{\gamma^{2}(\zeta-1)-\zeta}{b^{2}}K\left(-\frac{y^{2}}{b^{2}}\right)-\frac{\gamma^{2}(\zeta-2)-(\zeta-1)}{b^{2}+y^{2}}E\left(-\frac{y^{2}}{b^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{=asdf}+(1\leftrightarrow 2)\,,

(262)

which, unlike the aligned-spin case (
260
), develops a
logarithmic
singularity at
b^{2}=0
and another singularity at the unphysical impact parameter
b^{2}=-y^{2}
. The results (
260
) and (
262
) can be reproduced from the full eikonal given in the ancillary file
eikonaldata.dat.m
by taking the corresponding configurations and resumming the series expansion in
y^{2}/b^{2}
.

Before ending this section, we remark that the LSZ-like formulae (
233
) and (
234
) can also be applied to 2PL scattering observables, where retarded worldline propagators are used instead
Jakobsen:2022psy
. The 2PL observables can be separated into the
eikonal part
(the same diagrams with symmetric worldline
i0^{+}
prescription) and the
causality cut part
(the contributions from changing the worldline
i0^{+}
prescription), where the eikonal part computes
\{\chi_{(2)},O\}
and the causality cut part computes
\frac{1}{2}\{\chi_{(1)},\{\chi_{(1)},O\}\}
. This computation serves as a consistency check of the calculations in section
3.2.2
. The separation of the observables into the eikonal part and the causality cut part can be shown to be a more general phenomenon that holds in Hamiltonian worldline models
Kim:causality-cut
.

7
Discussion

The (ambi-)twistor model for electromagnetically interacting spinning particles was studied in this manuscript, which has the advantage that it is one of the simplest descriptions of charged spinning particles where spin effects can be tracked to arbitrarily high orders. Using (dynamical) Newman-Janis shift as the only input for generating all-orders-in-spin interactions, it was found that the spin effects can be resummed to simple expressions in special kinematic configurations; in the aligned-spin case (
260
) and in the axial scattering case (
262
). Also, the model was used to confirm the interpretation, up to the 2PL order, of the classical eikonal as the generator of canonical transformations that map the incoming scattering states to outgoing scattering states.

Despite the disparities between electromagnetic and gravitational interactions, the similarities between the singularity structures of the spin-resummed electromagnetic eikonal (
260
),
\chi_{(2)}\propto(b^{2}-(a_{1}+a_{2})^{2})^{-3/2}
, and the probe limit Kerr scattering reported by ref.
Damgaard:2022jem
,
\theta_{(2)}\sim\partial_{b}\chi_{(2)}\propto\partial_{b}[(b^{2}-a_{b}^{2})^{-3/2}]
, provides further evidence that using the total spin length vector
a_{+}^{\mu}=a_{1}^{\mu}+a_{2}^{\mu}
as the spin parameter of the effective Kerr metric—an ansatz motivated by leading order PN Hamiltonian results
Vines:2016qwa
—in the effective-one-body approach
Khalil:2023kep
is the preferable choice for resumming spin effects.
17
17

17

This is not the unique choice considered in the literature. A comparison of different choices for the spin parameter of the effective Kerr metric can be found in ref.
Khalil:2020mmr
.
On the other hand, one-loop results only correspond to leading order effects in the mass-ratio expansion
Vines:2018gqi
, therefore the singularity structures resembling that of the background-probe calculation
Damgaard:2022jem
could be a coincidence of the leading order mass-ratio expansion. Whether novel singularity structures arise at NLO in mass-ratio expansion will only be answered by pushing the computations to two-loops and higher orders, and may point us to new directions in resumming spin effects. Of course, studying the gravitationally interacting case is also necessary to confirm that such singularity structures are also present in gravitating binary black holes.

When viewing the classical eikonal as the generator of canonical transformations, it would be interesting to understand what it means to analytically continue the scattering generator to bound dynamics. The boundary-to-bound map for the radial action
Kalin:2019rwq
;
Kalin:2019inp
suggests that the continuation is a finite time-evolution generator that advances the system by one radial period, e.g. the periastron passing is sent to the next periastron passing. If this interpretation is correct, then we may argue that separability of the Hamilton-Jacobi equations is not necessary for the existence of the bound orbit counterpart of the classical eikonal, although its determination by analytic methods may only be possible when Hamilton-Jacobi equations are separable
Gonzo:2023goe
;
Gonzo:2024zxo
.

Apart from the obvious future direction—massive twistor worldline in gravitational fields—there are several other directions that would be interesting to expand upon. One future research direction would be to explore whether recent attempts to resum analytic results for gravitational scattering of spinning black holes
Rettegno:2023ghr
;
Buonanno:2024vkx
can be improved using the singularity structures of (
260
) and their conjectured generalisation to higher loops
\chi_{(n)}\propto(b^{2}-(a_{1}+a_{2})^{2})^{-3n/2}
.

Another direction would be making the (WQFT approach to the) model live up to its name;
quantisation
. Since the twistor model has a simple set of constraints, the standard BRST-BFV methods should be applicable. For small values of quantised spin, say
1/2
or
1
, we expect the results to agree with the standard QFT of massive spinning fields. The attempt to quantise the model for higher spin may shed new light on the complication with massive higher spin fields. Comparison of the approach with chiral models for massive higher spin fields
Ochirov:2022nqz
would also be an interesting study.

While the fundamental variables of our model are twistors, the physical observables (and the classical eikonal) were given entirely in terms of the gauge invariant
(x,y,p)
variables. Some intermediate steps of the computations, such as the ones in appendix
C
, tend to be quite lengthy and not particularly illuminating.
The computations may become vastly simplified when full advantage of the twistor variables is taken. To do so, it would be crucial to use massless twistor variables
for the photon fields as well. Bailey’s twistor propagator
Bailey:1985a
, and Guevara’s holomorphic classical limit
Guevara:2017csg
and twistor reconstruction
Guevara:2021yud
could provide clues for further progress.

We remark that iterated action of the classical eikonal can be understood as causality cuts, which computes contributions associated to changing the
i0^{+}
prescription of the worldline propagators from time-symmetric to retarded; in the WQFT formalism scattering observables are computed using retarded propagators
Jakobsen:2022psy
, and changing the
i0^{+}
prescription of the worldline propagators from retarded to symmetric generates (nested) Poisson brackets which reorganises the scattering observable
\Delta O
as the action of the scattering generator
e^{\{\chi,\bullet\}}O

Kim:causality-cut
.
A direct consequence is that the longitudinal impulse at 2PL order is related to the
i0^{+}
prescription of the worldline propagators, which could be an interesting observation for understanding the
i0^{+}
prescription affecting the definition of the impact parameter used in one-loop waveform results
Brandhuber:2023hhy
;
Herderschee:2023fxh
;
Elkhidir:2023dco
;
Georgoudis:2023lgf
;
Caron-Huot:2023vxl
;
Bini:2023fiz
;
Georgoudis:2023eke
.

Finally, it would be interesting to generalise the concept of the classical eikonal to massless fields.
Such an extended eikonal would place massive particles and massless fields on an equal footing,
and may help us clarify to what extent we can identify the eikonal as the classical shadow of the quantum
S
-matrix.

Appendix A
Conventions

Vector

Flat metric and Levi-Civita tensor,

\displaystyle\eta_{\mu\nu}=\mathrm{diag}(-,+,+,+)\,,\quad\varepsilon_{0123}=+1\,.

(263)

Electromagnetism without spin,

\displaystyle\partial^{\mu}F_{\mu\nu}=-J_{\nu}\,,\quad m\frac{du^{\mu}}{d\tau}=qF^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}\,.

(264)

Hodge star acting on a two-form,

\displaystyle(*F)_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}F^{\rho\sigma}\,.

(265)

Self-dual and anti-self-dual parts of a two-form,

\displaystyle F^{\pm}=\frac{1}{2}(F\mp i\!*\!F)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad*(F^{\pm})=\pm i(F^{\pm})\,.

(266)

We may also use
\tilde{F}_{\mu\nu}=(*F)_{\mu\nu}
.
If we define
A^{\pm}
and
\tilde{A}
by
F^{\pm}=dA^{\pm}
and
\tilde{F}=d\tilde{A}
,

\displaystyle A_{\mu}=A^{+}_{\mu}+A^{-}_{\mu}\,,\quad\tilde{A}_{\mu}=i(A^{+}_{\mu}-A^{-}_{\mu})\,.

(267)

Spinor

We follow the conventions of ref.
Kim:2021rda
to a large extent, where
|\lambda\rangle
spinors are associated to
incoming
negative helicity states.
An important difference is that we define

\displaystyle v_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}:=v_{\mu}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}\,,\quad v^{\mu}=-\frac{1}{2}(\bar{\sigma}^{\mu})^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha}v_{\alpha{\dot{\alpha}}}

(268)

for
all
vectorial quantities, nullifying the exception for
x^{\mu}
made in ref.
Kim:2021rda
.
To compare with references where the metric
\eta^{\mu\nu}
and/or
the Levi-Civita tensor
\varepsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}
carry the opposite sign (
e.g.
ref.
Chung:2018kqs
),
an invariant way to express conversion between spinor and Lorentz indices is to introduce the parameters
\kappa_{0}=\eta_{00}
and
\kappa_{1}=\varepsilon_{0123}
:

\displaystyle p_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=p_{\mu}\sigma^{\mu}_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=\left\{\begin{aligned} &\kappa_{0}|p\rangle_{\alpha}[p|_{\dot{\alpha}}&&p^{2}=0\\
&\kappa_{0}|p^{I}\rangle_{\alpha}[p_{I}|_{\dot{\alpha}}&&p^{2}=\kappa_{0}m^{2}\end{aligned}\right.

(269)

\displaystyle v_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}w^{\dot{\alpha}\alpha}

\displaystyle=2\kappa_{0}(v\cdot w)

(270)

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\mu}\bar{\sigma}^{\nu}\sigma^{\lambda}\bar{\sigma}^{\sigma})_{\alpha}{}^{\alpha}

\displaystyle=2(\eta^{\mu\nu}\eta^{\lambda\sigma}-\eta^{\mu\lambda}\eta^{\nu\sigma}+\eta^{\mu\sigma}\eta^{\nu\lambda}-i\kappa_{1}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\lambda\sigma})

(271)

The invariant tensor satisfies the complex conjugation relation

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\dot{\alpha}\dot{\gamma}}\epsilon^{\beta\delta}\left[\sigma^{\mu}_{\gamma\dot{\delta}}\right]^{\ast}=\bar{\sigma}^{\mu\dot{\alpha}\beta}

(272)

which is useful for evaluating complex conjugation of vectors.

Twistor

The two major differences from ref.
Kim:2021rda
are

\displaystyle(x^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha})_{\mathrm{here}}=(-2)(x^{{\dot{\alpha}}\alpha})_{\mathrm{there}}\,,\quad(\mu^{\dot{\alpha}I},\bar{\mu}_{I}{}^{\alpha})_{\mathrm{here}}=-(\mu^{\dot{\alpha}I},\bar{\mu}_{I}{}^{\alpha})_{\mathrm{there}}\,.

(273)

These changes propagate to all other equations. For example,
the incidence relations read

\displaystyle\mu^{\dot{\alpha}I}=\frac{1}{2}z^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}\lambda_{\beta}{}^{I}\,,\quad\bar{\mu}_{I}{}^{\alpha}=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\lambda}_{I\dot{\beta}}\bar{z}^{\dot{\beta}\alpha}\,,

(274)

where we define the complex conjugate relations as

\displaystyle\bar{z}^{\mu}=\left[z^{\mu}\right]^{\ast}\Rightarrow\bar{z}^{\dot{\alpha}\beta}=\left[z^{\dot{\beta}\alpha}\right]^{\ast}\,,\quad\bar{\mu}_{I}{}^{\alpha}=\left[\mu^{\dot{\alpha}I}\right]^{\ast}\,,\,\bar{\lambda}_{I\dot{\alpha}}=\left[\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\right]^{\ast}\,.

(275)

The defining Poisson brackets are

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\{x^{\mu},p_{\nu}\}=\delta^{\mu}_{\nu}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\{x^{\dot{\alpha}\alpha},p_{\beta\dot{\beta}}\}=-2\delta^{\dot{\alpha}}_{\dot{\beta}}\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\,,\\
\{\bar{\mu}_{I}{}^{\alpha},\lambda_{\beta}{}^{J}\}=\delta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\delta_{I}^{J}\,,\quad\{\mu^{\dot{\alpha}I},\bar{\lambda}_{J\dot{\beta}}\}=\delta_{\dot{\beta}}^{\dot{\alpha}}\delta^{I}_{J}\,.\end{gathered}

The consistency of the defining brackets can be confirmed from the relations

\displaystyle x^{\dot{\alpha}\alpha}=\frac{z^{\dot{\alpha}\alpha}+\bar{z}^{\dot{\alpha}\alpha}}{2}\,,\quad p_{\alpha\dot{\alpha}}=-\lambda_{\alpha}{}^{I}\bar{\lambda}_{I\dot{\alpha}}\,.

(278)

To determine the relation between
y^{\mu}
and the spin-length vector
a^{\mu}=S^{\mu}/m
, we note the Poisson brackets of the rotation generators

\displaystyle\{J^{23},J^{31}\}=J^{12}\;\;\Leftrightarrow\;\;\{J^{1},J^{2}\}=J^{3}

(279)

and leverage the calculation to demand that

\displaystyle\{S^{\mu},S^{\nu}\}_{\ast}=(-\kappa_{1})\varepsilon^{\alpha\mu\nu\lambda}\frac{(-p_{\alpha})}{m}S_{\lambda}\;\;\Leftrightarrow\;\;\{a^{\mu},a^{\nu}\}_{\ast}=\frac{\kappa_{1}}{m^{2}}\varepsilon^{\alpha\mu\nu\lambda}p_{\alpha}a_{\lambda}

(280)

where
\{\bullet,\bullet\}_{\ast}
is the Dirac bracket and
\kappa_{1}=\varepsilon_{0123}
.
The end result is the standard convention for the orientation of
a^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\{a^{1},a^{2}\}_{*}=+\frac{a^{3}}{m}\,.

(281)

On the twistor side, from the Poisson brackets we find

\displaystyle\{z^{\mu},\bar{z}^{\nu}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{-2i}{m}\left[y^{\mu}v^{\nu}-\eta^{\mu\nu}(y\cdot v)+v^{\mu}y^{\mu}+i\kappa_{1}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}y_{\alpha}v_{\beta}\right]

(282)

which implies

\displaystyle\{y^{\mu},y^{\nu}\}=\frac{\kappa_{1}}{m}\varepsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}y_{\alpha}v_{\beta}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\{y^{1},y^{2}\}=-\kappa_{1}\varepsilon^{1230}\frac{y^{3}}{m}=-\frac{y^{3}}{m}\,.

(283)

Thus we have to set
y^{\mu}=-a^{\mu}
.

Appendix B
List of integrals

B.1
Master one-loop integral

We compute the Euclidean loop integral
(k\cdot v=0)

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})^{\lambda_{1}}[(k-\ell_{E})^{2}]^{\lambda_{2}}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})^{\lambda_{3}}}\,,

using the identities

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \frac{1}{\alpha^{\lambda}}&=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\lambda)}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\,t^{\lambda-1}e^{-\alpha t}\,,\\
\frac{1}{(\alpha-i0^{+})^{\lambda}}&=\frac{i^{\lambda}}{\Gamma(\lambda)}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt\,t^{\lambda-1}e^{-i(\alpha-i0^{+})t}\,.\end{aligned}

(284)

After substitution, we have the integral

\displaystyle\frac{i^{\lambda_{3}}}{\Gamma(\lambda_{1})\Gamma(\lambda_{2})\Gamma(\lambda_{3})}\int_{0}^{\infty}dt_{1}dt_{2}dt_{3}\,t_{1}^{\lambda_{1}-1}t_{2}^{\lambda_{2}-1}t_{3}^{\lambda_{3}-1}\exp\left[-\frac{t_{1}t_{2}}{t_{1}+t_{2}}k^{2}-\frac{t_{3}^{2}}{t_{1}+t_{2}}v^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\phantom{asdfas}\times\exp\left[\frac{2t_{2}}{t_{1}+t_{2}}(k\cdot a)+\frac{-2it_{3}}{t_{1}+t_{2}}(v\cdot a)+\frac{a^{2}}{t_{1}+t_{2}}\right]\int_{\ell_{E}^{\prime}}e^{-(t_{1}+t_{2})(\ell_{E}^{\prime})^{2}}

where
\ell_{E}^{\prime}
is the shifted loop integration variable. We perform the Gaussian integral and expand the exponential of the second line. Evaluating the gamma function and beta function integrals, we get

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mu^{2\epsilon}d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})^{\lambda_{1}}[(k-\ell_{E})^{2}]^{\lambda_{2}}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})^{\lambda_{3}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{i^{\lambda_{3}}\pi^{D/2}\mu^{2\epsilon}}{2\Gamma(\lambda_{1})\Gamma(\lambda_{2})\Gamma(\lambda_{3})(k^{2})^{\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}-\frac{D}{2}}(v^{2})^{\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\times\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(-2i(v\cdot a)\sqrt{\frac{k^{2}}{v^{2}}}\right)^{m}(k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{l!m!n!}\frac{\Gamma(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}-\frac{m}{2}-n-\frac{D}{2})\Gamma(\frac{\lambda_{3}+m}{2})}{\Gamma(D-\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{3}+l+m+2n)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=asdfasdf}\times\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda_{2}-\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}+\frac{m}{2}+n)\,\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda_{1}-\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}+l+\frac{m}{2}+n)\,,

(285)

where
\mu^{2\epsilon}
is the mass scale required for dimensional regularisation
D=3-2\epsilon
. It is easy to verify that for
\lambda_{1}=0
or
\lambda_{2}=0
the integral vanishes, and for
\lambda_{3}=0
that the integral localises onto
m=0
.
18
18

18

The value for
\lambda_{3}=0
should be understood as a limiting value
\lambda_{3}\to 0
, where
\frac{\Gamma(\lambda_{3}/2)}{\Gamma(\lambda_{3})}\to 2
.
Setting
a=0
we only keep
l=m=n=0
of the sum, and recover the Euclidean version of (10.25) of ref.
Smirnov:2012gma
. The divergence of the integral for non-positive integral values of
(\lambda_{1}+\lambda_{2}+\frac{\lambda_{3}}{2}-\frac{m}{2}-n-\frac{D}{2})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\leq 0}
is harmless since the result formally becomes non-negative integral powers of
k^{2}
, which vanishes under the impact parameter space integral
\int_{k}e^{ik\cdot b}
for
b^{\mu}\neq 0
. The master integral (
285
) can be viewed as a
tensor integral generating function
, e.g. the vector integral can be evaluated as

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,\ell_{E}^{\mu}\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})^{\lambda_{1}}[(k-\ell_{E})^{2}]^{\lambda_{2}}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})^{\lambda_{3}}}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\partial}{\partial a_{\mu}}\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})^{\lambda_{1}}[(k-\ell_{E})^{2}]^{\lambda_{2}}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})^{\lambda_{3}}}\,.

This can be used to check consistency of (
285
), e.g.
v^{\mu}\frac{\partial}{\partial a^{\mu}}\Leftrightarrow(\lambda_{3}\to\lambda_{3}-1)
. Such consistency relations could be used to bootstrap tensor integral generating functions
Feng:2022hyg
.

In all the integrals listed in this appendix, we only keep the non-analytic terms in
k^{2}
and drop dimensional regularisation artefacts (
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon^{-1})
and
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
). All
\log(k^{2})
-dependent terms of the integrals vanish for time-symmetric
i0^{+}
prescription, which is equivalent to taking the real part of the integrals.

Special cases: Scalar integrals

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1
,
\lambda_{3}=2
, and
D=3-2\epsilon
with extra
k^{2}
.

\displaystyle k^{2}\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})(k-\ell_{E})^{2}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{3}}{2(k^{2})^{1/2}v^{2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n-\frac{1}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+\frac{1}{2})\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n-1)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{i\pi^{2}(v\cdot a)\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{3/2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n)(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{5/2}}{2(k^{2})^{1/2}v^{2}}\sum_{l,m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m-\frac{1}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{m}\,{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-m;\frac{1}{2};\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}a^{2}})}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(l+2m-1)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{i\pi^{2}(v\cdot a)\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{3/2}}\sum_{l,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+n)(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}\left(1-\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{a^{2}v^{2}}\right)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2n)}\,.

(286)

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{3}=1
and
D=3-2\epsilon
with extra
k^{2}
.

\displaystyle k^{2}\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,\mu^{2\epsilon}e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})(k-\ell_{E})^{2}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{3}(v\cdot a)(k^{2})^{1/2}}{2v^{2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n+\frac{1}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+\frac{3}{2})\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n+1)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{i\pi^{2}\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{1/2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n)(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{5/2}(v\cdot a)(k^{2})^{1/2}}{v^{2}}\sum_{l,m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+\frac{1}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{m}\,{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-m;\frac{3}{2};\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}a^{2}})}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(l+2m+1)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\frac{i\pi^{2}\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{1/2}}\sum_{l,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+n)(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}\left(1-\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{a^{2}v^{2}}\right)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2n)}\,.

(287)

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1
,
\lambda_{3}=0
, and

.

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})(k-\ell_{E})^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\pi^{5/2}}{(k^{2})^{1/2}}\sum_{l,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+n+\frac{1}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2n+1)}\,.

(288)

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,(2v\cdot\ell_{E})\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})(k-\ell_{E})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-2\pi^{5/2}(v\cdot a)(k^{2})^{1/2}\sum_{l,m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+\frac{3}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{m}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(l+2m+3)}\,.

Special cases: Vector integrals

Assume
f\cdot k=f\cdot v=0
.
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=\lambda_{3}=1
and
D=3-2\epsilon
.

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,\mu^{2\epsilon}(f\cdot\ell_{E})\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})(k-\ell_{E})^{2}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi^{3}(v\cdot a)(f\cdot a)(k^{2})^{1/2}}{2v^{2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n+\frac{3}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+\frac{3}{2})\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n+3)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{i\pi^{2}(f\cdot a)\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{1/2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n+1)(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n+2)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi^{5/2}(v\cdot a)(f\cdot a)(k^{2})^{1/2}}{v^{2}}\sum_{l,m=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+\frac{3}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{m}\,{}_{2}F_{1}(1,-m;\frac{3}{2};\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}a^{2}})}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(l+2m+3)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{i\pi^{2}(f\cdot a)\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{1/2}}\sum_{l,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+n+1)(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}\left(1-\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{a^{2}v^{2}}\right)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2n+2)}\,.

(290)

\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=1
,
\lambda_{3}=2
, and
D=3-2\epsilon
.

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}\ell_{E}\,\,\mu^{2\epsilon}(f\cdot\ell_{E})\,e^{2\ell_{E}\cdot a}}{(\ell_{E}^{2})(k-\ell_{E})^{2}(2v\cdot\ell_{E}-i0^{+})^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\pi^{3}(f\cdot a)}{2(k^{2})^{1/2}v^{2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n+\frac{3}{2})(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+\frac{1}{2})\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n+3)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{i\pi^{2}(f\cdot a)(v\cdot a)\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{3/2}}\sum_{l,m,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+m+n+1)(2k\cdot a)^{l}\left(k^{2}\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{v^{2}}\right)^{m}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(m+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2m+2n+2)}
