\displaystyle\phantom{=}-\frac{i\pi^{2}(f\cdot a)(v\cdot a)\log(k^{2})}{2(v^{2})^{3/2}}\sum_{l,n=0}^{\infty}\frac{\Gamma(l+n+1)(2k\cdot a)^{l}(-k^{2}a^{2})^{n}\left(1-\frac{(v\cdot a)^{2}}{a^{2}v^{2}}\right)^{n}}{\Gamma(l+1)\Gamma(n+1)\Gamma(l+2n+2)}\,.

(291)

B.2
Fourier transform integral

The Fourier transform to impact parameter space is given as

\displaystyle\int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{e^{i(k_{E}\cdot b)}}{[k_{E}^{2}\mp i0^{+}]^{\lambda}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda)}{2^{2\lambda}\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}\Gamma(\lambda)}\frac{1}{\left(b^{2}\pm i0^{+}\right)^{\frac{D}{2}-\lambda}}\,,

(292)

where we assumed
b^{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and included
i0^{+}
prescription for convergence. The
i0^{+}
prescription can be dropped since there is no branch cut ambiguity for Euclidean signature.

Fourier transforms with numerators can be evaluated using differentiation;
k_{E}^{\mu}\leftrightarrow-i\frac{\partial}{\partial b_{\mu}}
. Repeated numerator factors can be computed as directional derivatives, i.e.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{(k_{E}\cdot a)^{l}e^{i(k_{E}\cdot b)}}{[k_{E}^{2}]^{\lambda}}&=l!\left.\int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{e^{i(k_{E}\cdot[b-ia])}}{[k_{E}^{2}]^{\lambda}}\right|_{\mathcal{O}(a^{l})}\\
&=\left.\frac{l!\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda)}{2^{2\lambda}\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}\Gamma(\lambda)}\frac{1}{\left[(b-ia)^{2}\right]^{\frac{D}{2}-\lambda}}\right|_{\mathcal{O}(a^{l})}\\
&=\frac{l!\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda)}{2^{2\lambda}\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}\Gamma(\lambda)}\frac{i^{l}C_{l}^{(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda)}(\frac{(a\cdot b)}{(a^{2}b^{2})^{1/2}})(a^{2})^{l/2}}{\left(b^{2}\right)^{\frac{D}{2}-\lambda+\frac{l}{2}}}\,,\end{aligned}

(293)

where
C_{n}^{(\lambda)}(x)
is the ultraspherical/Gegenbauer polynomial. Differentiation in
\lambda
can be used to compute Fourier transform for logarithms,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}[k_{E}^{2}]^{\lambda}(k_{E}\cdot a)^{l}\log(k_{E}^{2})e^{i(k_{E}\cdot b)}=l!\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda}\int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}[k_{E}^{2}]^{\lambda}e^{i(k_{E}\cdot[b-ia])}\right|_{\mathcal{O}(a^{l})}\\
&\phantom{asdfasdf}=-\frac{(-4)^{\lambda}\lambda!\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}+\lambda)\Gamma(l+1)}{\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}}\frac{i^{l}C_{l}^{(\frac{D}{2}+\lambda)}(\frac{(a\cdot b)}{(a^{2}b^{2})^{1/2}})(a^{2})^{l/2}}{\left(b^{2}\right)^{\frac{D}{2}+\lambda+\frac{l}{2}}}\,,\end{aligned}

(294)

where
\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
is assumed.

Special cases: aligned spin

Only even powers of
l
are relevant for the aligned spin configuration, where
a\cdot b=0
. The master integral (
293
) reduces to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{(k_{E}\cdot a)^{2l}e^{i(k_{E}\cdot b)}}{[k_{E}^{2}]^{\lambda}}&=\frac{\Gamma(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda)}{2^{2\lambda}\pi^{\frac{D}{2}}\Gamma(\lambda)}\frac{(\frac{D}{2}-\lambda)_{l}}{\left(b^{2}\right)^{\frac{D}{2}-\lambda}}\frac{(2l)!}{l!}\left(\frac{a^{2}}{b^{2}}\right)^{l}\,,\end{aligned}

(295)

where
(a)_{n}
is the Pochhammer symbol. For axial scattering we set
a\cdot b=\pm\sqrt{a^{2}b^{2}}
, which leads to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int\frac{d^{D}k_{E}}{(2\pi)^{D}}\frac{(k_{E}\cdot a)^{2l}e^{i(k_{E}\cdot b)}}{[k_{E}^{2}]^{\lambda}}&=\frac{\Gamma(D+2l-2\lambda)}{2^{D-1}\pi^{\frac{D-1}{2}}\Gamma(\lambda)\Gamma(\frac{D+1}{2}-\lambda)}\frac{1}{\left(b^{2}\right)^{\frac{D}{2}-\lambda}}\left(\frac{-a^{2}}{b^{2}}\right)^{l}\,.\end{aligned}

(296)

Appendix C
2PL computations

C.1
Longitudinal part of the momentum kick

Our goal is to show that the longitudinal part of
\Delta_{(2)}p_{1}^{\mu}

agrees with

\displaystyle\Delta_{(2)}p_{1}^{\mu}|_{\mathrm{iter}}=\frac{1}{2}\{\chi_{(1)},\{\chi_{(1)},p_{1}^{\mu}\}\}=\frac{1}{2}\{\chi_{(1)},\Delta_{(1)}p_{1}^{\mu}\}\,.

(297)

Part 1

In the spin-less case, all iteration terms contain
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}
. That is no longer true when spin is turned on.
Let us first focus on the new terms not containing
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}
.

Using the following results as building blocks,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{k\cdot b,\ell\cdot b\}=\{k\cdot y,\ell\cdot y\}&=\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{2},v_{2}]}{m_{2}}\,,\\
\left\{k\cdot b,-\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\right\}&=\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{1}]}{m_{2}}\,,\\
\left\{\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y],\ell\cdot b\right\}&=\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{1}]}{m_{2}}\,,\\
\left\{\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y],\ell\cdot y\right\}&=(k\cdot\ell)\left(\frac{v_{2}\cdot y_{1}}{m_{1}}-\frac{v_{1}\cdot y_{2}}{m_{2}}\right)\,,\end{split}

(298)

we collect four contributions to (
297
):

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{1}^{\mu}&=(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma^{2}\left\{\cosh(k\cdot y)e^{ik\cdot b},\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{i\ell\cdot b}\right\}\\
&=(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma^{2}\{k\cdot y,\ell\cdot y\}\sinh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad-(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma^{2}\{k\cdot b,\ell\cdot b\}\cosh(k\cdot y)\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\end{split}

(299)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{4}^{\mu}&=\left\{-i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}e^{ik\cdot b},-\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{i\ell\cdot b}\right\}\\
&=i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\{k\cdot y,\ell\cdot y\}\left[\frac{\cosh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}-\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{2}}\right]\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad-i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\{k\cdot b,\ell\cdot b\}\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{1}]}{m_{2}}\right)\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad-\left(\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{1}]}{m_{2}}\right)\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i(k\cdot\ell)\left(\frac{v_{2}\cdot y_{1}}{m_{1}}-\frac{v_{1}\cdot y_{2}}{m_{2}}\right)\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\end{split}

(300)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{2}^{\mu}&=\gamma\left\{\cosh(k\cdot y)e^{ik\cdot b},-\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{i\ell\cdot b}\right\}\\
&=-\gamma\,\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\{k\cdot y,\ell\cdot y\}\sinh(k\cdot y)\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+\gamma\,\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\{k\cdot b,\ell\cdot b\}\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i\gamma\left(\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{1}]}{m_{2}}\right)\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\end{split}

(301)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{3}^{\mu}&=(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma\left\{-i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}e^{ik\cdot b},\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{i\ell\cdot b}\right\}\\
&=\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\{k\cdot y,\ell\cdot y\}\left[\frac{\cosh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}-\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{2}}\right]\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad-\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\{k\cdot b,\ell\cdot b\}\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)\left(\frac{v_{2}\cdot y_{1}}{m_{1}}-\frac{v_{1}\cdot y_{2}}{m_{2}}\right)\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\left(\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]}{m_{1}}+\frac{\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{1}]}{m_{2}}\right)\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\end{split}

(302)

As usual, we may work in the probe limit
(m_{1}/m_{2}\rightarrow 0)
, where we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}A_{1}^{\mu}&=-(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma^{2}\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]\cosh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\\
m_{1}A_{4}^{\mu}&=-i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{2}}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]\frac{\cosh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]}{k\cdot y}e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad-\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i(k\cdot\ell)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\end{split}

(303)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}A_{2}^{\mu}&=-\gamma\,\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]\sinh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i\gamma\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\\
m_{1}A_{3}^{\mu}&=-\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{(k\cdot y)^{2}}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad-\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]\frac{\sinh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]}{k\cdot y}e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad+i\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\,.\end{split}

(304)

We can simplify
A_{2}^{\mu}
a bit and write

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}A_{2}^{\mu}&=-\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\sinh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\;+i\gamma\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\;}\,,\end{split}

(305)

Next, using (
104
), we try to remove
(k\cdot y)
factors in the denominators of
A_{3}^{\mu}
and
A_{4}^{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}A_{3}^{\mu}&=-\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\sinh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\;+i\gamma\,\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\;}\,,\\
m_{1}A_{4}^{\mu}&=(i\ell^{\mu})(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\cosh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\\
&\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\;+\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\;}\\
&\quad{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\;-\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)e^{iq\cdot b}\;}\,.\end{split}

(306)

We have enumerated all terms
not
containing
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}
.
Now we split them into two parts: the “y-part” and the “v-part”. The former is linear in
y_{1}
while the latter (marked red in the equations above) is independent of
y_{1}
. The
k\cdot y
factor in the denominator is to be cancelled against an
\epsilon[\cdot,\cdot,\cdot,y]
factor in the numerator.

The y-part gives fairly simple expressions:

\displaystyle\begin{split}m_{1}A_{1^{+}4}^{\mu}|_{y}&=(i\ell^{\mu})(-\gamma^{2}\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{1}]+(v_{2}\cdot y)\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}])\cosh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\,,\\
m_{1}A_{2+3}^{\mu}|_{y}&=(i\ell^{\mu})(2i\gamma)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})(k\cdot\ell)\sinh[(k-\ell)\cdot y]e^{iq\cdot b}\,.\end{split}

(307)

There agree perfectly with the longitudinal part of (
110
), (
112
) in the main text.

Part 2

The v-part is more involved, as it gets combined with the
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}
terms.
In the probe limit, the
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}
factor comes from

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left\{e^{ik\cdot b}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1}),e^{i\ell\cdot b}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(\ell\cdot v_{1})\right\}\;\rightarrow\;\;&i(k\cdot\ell)\frac{e^{iq\cdot b}}{m_{1}}\left[\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}(\ell\cdot v_{1})-\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}(k\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(\ell\cdot v_{1})\right]\\
&=i(k\cdot\ell)\frac{e^{iq\cdot b}}{m_{1}}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(q\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}(\ell\cdot v_{1})\,.\end{split}

(308)

This factor is to be multiplied by

\displaystyle\begin{split}B^{\mu}&=\left[\gamma\cosh(k\cdot y)-i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\right]\\
&\quad\times\left[(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma\cosh(\ell\cdot y)-\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\right]\\
&=B_{1}^{\mu}+B_{2}^{\mu}+B_{3}^{\mu}+B_{4}^{\mu}\,,\end{split}

(309)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}B_{1}^{\mu}&=(i\ell^{\mu})\gamma^{2}\cosh(k\cdot y)\cosh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
B_{4}^{\mu}&=i\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
B_{2}^{\mu}&=-\gamma\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
B_{3}^{\mu}&=\gamma\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)\,.\end{split}

(310)

Multiplying them by
i(k\cdot\ell)
, and hiding
e^{iq\cdot b}\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(q\cdot v_{1})\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt^{\prime}(\ell\cdot v_{1})
for now, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}D_{1}^{\mu}&=-\ell^{\mu}\gamma^{2}(k\cdot\ell)\cosh(k\cdot y)\cosh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
D_{4}^{\mu}&=-(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
D_{2}^{\mu}&=-i\gamma(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
D_{3}^{\mu}&=i\gamma\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)\,.\end{split}

(311)

Now, we bring the red colored terms from (
305
) and (
306
) and apply the identity

\displaystyle\delta(x)=-x\delta^{\prime}(x)\,.

(312)

After pulling out some overall factor, we record the results as

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{1}^{\mu}&=0\,,\\
C_{4}^{\mu}&=-(\ell\cdot v_{1})\epsilon[k,v_{1},v_{2},y]\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\\
&\quad-(k\cdot v_{1})\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
C_{2}^{\mu}&=-i\gamma(\ell\cdot v_{1})\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}]\cosh(k\cdot y)\sinh(\ell\cdot y)\,,\\
C_{3}^{\mu}&=+i\gamma(k\cdot v_{1})\ell^{\mu}\epsilon[k,\ell,y,v_{2}]\frac{\sinh(k\cdot y)}{k\cdot y}\cosh(\ell\cdot y)\,.\end{split}

(313)

For
C_{3}^{\mu}
and the second line of
C_{4}^{\mu}
, we used
\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(k\cdot v_{1}+\ell\cdot v_{1})
to replace
(\ell\cdot v_{1})
by
-(k\cdot v_{1})
.
Merging all the
C
-terms and the
D
-terms,
we obtain the final result in perfect agreement with the longitudinal part of
\Delta_{(2v)}p^{\mu}
in (
113
), (
114
) in the main text.

C.2
Transverse part of the spin kick

In section
3.2.2
of the main text, we showed how to compute the 2PL spin kick.
In this appendix, we give some details of the computation and confirm that
the transverse part of the spin kick agrees with the eikonal formula,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\{\chi_{(n)},y_{1}^{\mu}\}&=\frac{1}{m_{1}}\left[v_{1}^{\mu}y_{1}^{\nu}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}^{\nu}}+\epsilon^{\mu\nu}[v_{1},y_{1}]\frac{\partial}{\partial y_{1}^{\nu}}\right]\chi_{(n)}\,.\end{split}

(314)

We begin with the overall structure of the 2PL spin kick (
121
):

\displaystyle\Delta_{(2)}y_{1}^{\mu}=\frac{(q_{1}q_{2})^{2}}{m_{1}^{2}}\int_{q_{\perp}}e^{iq\cdot b}\int_{\ell}\frac{\delta\kern-1.99997pt\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\bar{}$}\kern 1.99997pt(v_{2}\cdot\ell)}{k^{2}\ell^{2}(ik\cdot v_{1}+0^{+})^{2}}\mathcal{N}^{\mu}\,.

(315)

The numerator
\mathcal{N}^{\mu}
can be computed separately for each term in (
120
).
For (a) and (b) terms,
we also distinguish the same/opposite helicity contributions.

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{N}^{\mu}_{(2ao)}=\left(\mathrm{ch}\!\boxminus C_{ao}+\mathrm{sh}\!\boxminus S_{ao}\right)^{\mu}\,,&\quad\mathcal{N}^{\mu}_{(2as)}=\left(\mathrm{ch}\!\boxplus C_{as}+\mathrm{sh}\!\boxplus S_{as}\right)^{\mu}\,,\\
\mathcal{N}^{\mu}_{(2bo)}=\left(\mathrm{ch}\!\boxminus C_{bo}+\mathrm{sh}\!\boxminus S_{bo}\right)^{\mu}\,,&\quad\mathcal{N}^{\mu}_{(2bs)}=\left(\mathrm{ch}\!\boxplus C_{bs}+\mathrm{sh}\!\boxplus S_{bs}\right)^{\mu}\,,\\
\mathcal{N}^{\mu}_{(2c)}=\left(\mathrm{ch}\!\boxminus C_{c}+\mathrm{sh}\!\boxminus S_{c}\right)^{\mu}\,,&\quad\mathcal{N}^{\mu}_{(2d)}=\left(\mathrm{ch}\!\boxminus C_{d}+\mathrm{sh}\!\boxminus S_{d}\right)^{\mu}\,,\end{split}

(316)

The same helicity terms are

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{as}&=\frac{1}{2}(ik\cdot v_{1})\left[k^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})-\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot y_{1})-y_{1}^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)\right]\,,\quad S^{\mu}_{as}=\frac{i}{2}(ik\cdot v_{1})\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,y_{1}]\,,\\
C^{\mu}_{bs}&=\frac{i}{2}\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\gamma(k\cdot\ell)+\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{2},y_{1}]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{bs}&=\frac{1}{2}\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]-\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{2},y_{1}]\gamma(k\cdot\ell)\,.\end{split}

(317)

Among the opposite helicity terms, (a) and (b) terms are linear in
y_{1}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{ao}&=\frac{1}{2}(ik\cdot v_{1})\left[-k^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})-\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot y_{1})+y_{1}^{\mu}(k\cdot\ell)+2v_{2}^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})(k\cdot\ell)\right]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{ao}&=\frac{i}{2}(ik\cdot v_{1})\left[v_{2}^{\mu}\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{2}]+\epsilon^{\mu}[k,\ell,v_{2}](v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\right]\,,\\
C^{\mu}_{bo}&=\frac{i}{2}\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\gamma(k\cdot\ell)-\frac{i}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{2},y_{1}]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{bo}&=\frac{1}{2}\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{2},y_{1}]\gamma(k\cdot\ell)\,,\end{split}

(318)

whereas (c), (d) terms are quadratic in
y_{1}
:

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{c}&=(ik\cdot v_{1})i\left[\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{2}]-\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{2},y_{1}](k\cdot\ell)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})\right]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{c}&=(ik\cdot v_{1})\left[\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right](k\cdot\ell)(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{2},y_{1}]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{2},y_{1}]\right]\,,\\
C^{\mu}_{d}&=(ik\cdot v_{1})^{2}\left[(\ell\cdot v_{1})(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})+\gamma(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{2},y_{1}]\\
&\qquad+(ik\cdot v_{1})^{2}\epsilon[\ell,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\left[k^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(k\cdot y_{1})\right]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{d}&=(ik\cdot v_{1})^{2}i\left[(\ell\cdot v_{1})(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})+\gamma(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\left[k^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(k\cdot y_{1})\right]\\
&\qquad-(ik\cdot v_{1})^{2}i\epsilon[\ell,v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\epsilon^{\mu}[k,v_{2},y_{1}]\,.\end{split}

(319)

Same helicity sector

The 2PL eikonal (
118
) contains a single term in the same helicity sector, so its contribution to the spin kick should be also quite simple. Indeed, after a lot of cancellations, we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{as}+C^{\mu}_{bs}&=-\frac{i}{2}(k\cdot\ell)\left[v^{\mu}_{1}(\ell\cdot y_{1})-{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}y^{\mu}_{1}(\ell\cdot v_{1})}\right]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{as}+S^{\mu}_{bs}&=-\frac{1}{2}(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[v_{1},y_{1},\ell]\,.\end{split}

(320)

Not all terms contribute to the transverse part;
\Delta_{(2)}y^{\mu}|_{\mathrm{tr}}
should be orthogonal to
y_{1}^{\mu}
. The non-orthogonal term, marked red in the equation above, is projected out upon symmetrisation under the exchange operation
k\leftrightarrow\ell
,

\displaystyle(\ell\cdot v_{1})(k\cdot\ell)\cosh\boxplus

\displaystyle\;\;\rightarrow\;\;(q\cdot v_{1})(k\cdot\ell)\cosh\boxplus=0\,.

(321)

The equality hold in the
(q_{\perp})
integral.
What is left after the symmetrisation is

\displaystyle C^{\mu}_{as+bs}|_{\mathrm{tr}}=-\frac{i}{2}(k\cdot\ell)v^{\mu}_{1}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\,,\quad S^{\mu}_{as+bs}|_{\mathrm{tr}}=-\frac{1}{2}(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[v_{1},y_{1},\ell]\,.

(322)

These match the expectation from the 2PL eikonal (
314
) perfectly.

Opposite helicity sector

We treat the (a)-(b) group and the (c)-(d) group separately.
In the (a)-(b) group, partial cancellations leave us with

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{ao}+C^{\mu}_{bo}&=i(k\cdot\ell)\gamma\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]+\frac{i}{2}(k\cdot\ell)v_{1}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\\
&\quad+i\ell^{\mu}(\ell\cdot v_{1})(k\cdot y_{1})-\frac{i}{2}(k\cdot\ell)(\ell\cdot v_{1}){\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\left[2v_{2}^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})+y_{1}^{\mu}\right]}\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{ao}+S^{\mu}_{bo}&=\left[\ell^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})\right]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]+\frac{1}{2}(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[\ell,v_{1},y_{1}]\\
&\quad+{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}v_{2}^{\mu}(\ell\cdot v_{1})\epsilon[k,\ell,y_{1},v_{2}]}\,.\end{split}

(323)

Again, the non-orthogonal terms, marked red in the equations above, are projected out upon symmetrisation under the exchange
k\leftrightarrow\ell
.
We are left with

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{ao+bo}|_{\mathrm{tr}}&=i(k\cdot\ell)\gamma\left[q^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(q\cdot y_{1})\right]+\frac{i}{2}(k\cdot\ell)v_{1}^{\mu}(q\cdot y_{1})\\
&\quad+\frac{i}{2}[(k-\ell)\cdot v_{1}]\left[k^{\mu}(\ell\cdot y_{1})-\ell^{\mu}(k\cdot y_{1})\right]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{ao+bo}|_{\mathrm{tr}}&=\left[q^{\mu}(v_{2}\cdot y_{1})-v_{2}^{\mu}(q\cdot y_{1})\right]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]-\frac{1}{2}(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[k-\ell,v_{1},y_{1}]\,.\end{split}

(324)

On the other hand, the transverse spin kick derived from the eikonal by (
314
) contains

\displaystyle\begin{split}C^{\mu}_{\chi}|_{\mathrm{tr}}&=i(q\cdot y_{1})v_{1}^{\mu}\left[-(\gamma^{2}-1/2)(k\cdot\ell)+(k\cdot v_{1})(\ell\cdot v_{1})\right]\\
&\quad+i\gamma\epsilon^{\mu}[k-\ell,v_{1},y_{1}]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\\
&\quad-i[(k-\ell)\cdot v_{1}]\epsilon^{\mu}[v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\\
&\quad-\frac{i}{2}[(k-\ell)\cdot v_{1}]\epsilon^{\mu\nu}[v_{1},y_{1}]\epsilon_{\nu}[k,\ell,v_{1}]\,,\\
S^{\mu}_{\chi}|_{\mathrm{tr}}&=-v_{1}^{\mu}(q\cdot y_{1})\gamma\epsilon[k,\ell,v_{1},v_{2}]\\
&\quad-(\gamma^{2}-1/2)(k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[k-\ell,v_{1},y_{1}]+\gamma[(k-\ell)\cdot v_{1}](k\cdot\ell)\epsilon^{\mu}[v_{1},v_{2},y_{1}]\,.\end{split}

(325)

Despite appearances, things do match as expected,

\displaystyle C^{\mu}_{ao+bo}|_{\mathrm{tr}}=C^{\mu}_{\chi}|_{\mathrm{tr}}\,.\quad S^{\mu}_{ao+bo}|_{\mathrm{tr}}=S^{\mu}_{\chi}|_{\mathrm{tr}}\,.

(326)

We can repeat the same exercise for the (c)-(d) group. The computations are even lengthier and not particularly illuminating,
so we omit the details here.

Appendix D
Regularisation for the product of time-symmetric Green’s functions

The usual time-symmetric
i0^{+}
prescription for
\omega^{-2}
propagators is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{(\omega+i0^{+})^{2}}+\frac{1}{(\omega-i0^{+})^{2}}\right)\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad-\frac{1}{2}|\sigma|\,,

(327)

where we use the positive frequency expansion
f(\sigma)=\int_{\omega}f(\omega)e^{-i\omega\sigma}
. The square of the time-symmetric
\omega^{-1}
propagator is given as

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\omega+i0^{+}}+\frac{1}{\omega-i0^{+}}\right)\right]^{2}\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad-\frac{1}{4}|\sigma|+\frac{e^{-0^{+}\times|\sigma|}}{40^{+}}=\frac{1}{40^{+}}-\frac{1}{2}|\sigma|+\mathcal{O}[(0^{+})^{1}]\,,

(328)

where we expanded the expression as a Laurent series in the regulator
0^{+}
and kept up to
\mathcal{O}[(0^{+})^{0}]
terms. Employing the “minimal subtraction” scheme for the regulator
0^{+}
, we throw out the divergent term in
0^{+}
and conclude that the propagators (
327
) and (
328
) are equivalent as distributions.

The reason (
328
) has a divergent contribution compared to (
327
) is because it should be understood as the convolution of the time-symmetric
\omega^{-1}
propagator in the time domain,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\omega+i0^{+}}+\frac{1}{\omega-i0^{+}}\right)\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad-\frac{i}{2}\text{sgn}(\sigma)\,,

(329)

where
\text{sgn}(\sigma)
is the sign function. Unlike the retarded/advanced Green’s functions given by the Heaviside step function, the convolution of (
329
) with itself diverges due to the “infinite volume” of the real line, which manifests itself as the
(0^{+})^{-1}
divergence in (
328
).
